</p><p>Statements on the canonisation issue made by the Vatican's <a href="/wiki/Cardinal_Secretary_of_State" title="Cardinal Secretary of State">Secretary of State</a>, Cardinal <a href="/wiki/Pietro_Parolin" title="Pietro Parolin">Pietro Parolin</a>, on a visit to Croatia in September 2020,<sup id="cite_ref-268" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-268">&#91;264&#93;</a></sup> were interpreted by Croatian commentators and prelates as a clear indication that there would be no progress under this Pope.<sup id="cite_ref-jutlistudruzio_269-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jutlistudruzio-269">&#91;265&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Legacy">Legacy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Aloysius_Stepinac&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Legacy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>On 14 February 1992, Croatian representative <a href="/wiki/Vladimir_%C5%A0eks" title="Vladimir Šeks">Vladimir Šeks</a> put forth a declaration in the <a href="/wiki/Croatian_Parliament" title="Croatian Parliament">Croatian Sabor</a> condemning the court decision and the process that led to it.<sup id="cite_ref-VladimirSeks_270-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VladimirSeks-270">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup> The declaration was passed, along with a similar one about the death of Croatian communist official <a href="/wiki/Andrija_Hebrang_(father)" class="mw-redirect" title="Andrija Hebrang (father)">Andrija Hebrang</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-VladimirSeks_270-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VladimirSeks-270">&#91;266&#93;</a></sup> The declaration states that the true reason of Stepinac's imprisonment was his pointing out many communist crimes and especially refusing to form a Croatian Catholic Church in <a href="/wiki/Schism_(religion)" class="mw-redirect" title="Schism (religion)">schism</a> with the <a href="/wiki/Pope" title="Pope">Pope</a>. The verdict has not been formally challenged nor overturned in any court between 1997 and 1999 while it was possible under Croatian law.<sup id="cite_ref-271" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-271">&#91;267&#93;</a></sup> In 1998, the <a href="/wiki/Croatian_National_Bank" title="Croatian National Bank">Croatian National Bank</a> released commemoratives 500 kuna gold and 150 kuna silver coins.<sup id="cite_ref-272" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-272">&#91;268&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2007, the municipality of Marija Bistrica began on a project called <i>Stepinac's Path</i>, which would build pilgrimage paths linking places significant to the cardinal: <a href="/wiki/Kra%C5%A1i%C4%87" title="Krašić">Krašić</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kaptol,_Zagreb" title="Kaptol, Zagreb">Kaptol</a> in <a href="/wiki/Zagreb" title="Zagreb">Zagreb</a>, <a href="/wiki/Medvednica" title="Medvednica">Medvednica</a>, Marija Bistrica, and <a href="/wiki/Lepoglava" title="Lepoglava">Lepoglava</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-273" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-273">&#91;269&#93;</a></sup> The Aloysius Stepinac Museum opened in Zagreb in 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-274" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-274">&#91;270&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Croatian <a href="/wiki/Football_(soccer)" class="mw-redirect" title="Football (soccer)">football</a> international <a href="/wiki/Dario_%C5%A0imi%C4%87" title="Dario Šimić">Dario Šimić</a> wore a T-shirt with Stepinac's image on it under his jersey during the country's <a href="/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2008" title="UEFA Euro 2008">UEFA Euro 2008</a> game against Poland, which he revealed after the game.<sup id="cite_ref-275" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-275">&#91;271&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2008, a total of 119 streets in Croatia were named after Alojzije Stepinac, making him the tenth most common person eponym of streets in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-276" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-276">&#91;272&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Nominations_to_Righteous_Among_the_Nations">Nominations to Righteous Among the Nations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Aloysius_Stepinac&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Nominations to Righteous Among the Nations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Alojzije_Stepinac_Novi_Zagreb.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Alojzije_Stepinac_Novi_Zagreb.jpg/220px-Alojzije_Stepinac_Novi_Zagreb.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Alojzije_Stepinac_Novi_Zagreb.jpg/330px-Alojzije_Stepinac_Novi_Zagreb.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/98/Alojzije_Stepinac_Novi_Zagreb.jpg/440px-Alojzije_Stepinac_Novi_Zagreb.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="1600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Alojzije_Stepinac_Novi_Zagreb.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A statue of Stepinac in Zagreb</div></div></div>
<p>Stepinac was unsuccessfully recommended on two occasions by two individual Croatian Jews to be added to the list of the <a href="/wiki/Righteous_Among_the_Nations" title="Righteous Among the Nations">Righteous Among the Nations</a>. <a href="/wiki/Amiel_Shomrony" title="Amiel Shomrony">Amiel Shomrony</a> (previously known in Croatia as Emil Schwarz), the secretary to the war-time head rabbi Miroslav Šalom Freiberger, nominated Stepinac in 1970. He was again nominated in 1994 by Igor Primorac. <a href="/wiki/Esther_Gitman" title="Esther Gitman">Esther Gitman</a>, a Jew from <a href="/wiki/Sarajevo" title="Sarajevo">Sarajevo</a> living in the USA who holds a PhD on the subject of the fate of Jews in the Independent State of Croatia, stated that, for her, "Stepinac is holy man who saved many Jews".<sup id="cite_ref-277" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-277">&#91;273&#93;</a></sup> However the reason stated by Yad Vashem for denying the requests were that the proposers were not themselves <a href="/wiki/The_Holocaust" title="The Holocaust">Holocaust</a> survivors,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2009)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> which is a requirement for inclusion in the list; and that "persons who assisted Jews and simultanesously collaborated or were linked with a fascist regime which took part in the Nazi-orchestrated persectution of Jews may be disqualified for the Righteous title" .<sup id="cite_ref-278" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-278">&#91;274&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Primary_sources">Primary sources</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Aloysius_Stepinac&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Primary sources">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Although Stepinac's life has been the subject of much writing, there are very few primary sources for researchers to draw upon, the main one being the <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=Katoli%C4%8Dki_List&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Katolički List (page does not exist)">Katolički List</a></i>, a diocesan weekly journal.
</p><p>Stepinac's diary, discovered in 1950 (too late to be used in his trial), was confiscated by the Yugoslav authorities. Franjo Tudjman returned the diary to the Church in the early 1990s. Croatian historian, Zvonimir Despot, notes that 30 year's later the Church has still not published the diary. Juraj Batelja, the official Church <a href="/wiki/Postulator" title="Postulator">Postulator</a> advocating Stepinac's canonization, has for years selectively used parts of the diary for his books, without other researchers having an opportunity to see the original text. Despot notes that Stepinac did not write the diary as a private person, but as the Archbishop, and the diary is an extremely important source for Croatian history. Despot concludes by asking, “why is the diary kept secret to this day, under lock and key? Who is hiding what and why?”<sup id="cite_ref-279" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-279">&#91;275&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Father <a href="/wiki/Josip_Vrankovi%C4%87" title="Josip Vranković">Josip Vranković</a> kept a diary from December 1951 to 10 February 1960, recording what Stepinac related to him each day; that diary was used by Franciscan <a href="/w/index.php?title=Aleksa_Benigar&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Aleksa Benigar (page does not exist)">Aleksa Benigar</a> to write a biography of Stepinac, but Benigar refused to share the diary with any other researcher.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAlexander1987vii_280-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlexander1987vii-280">&#91;276&#93;</a></sup> The diocesan archives were also made available to Benigar, but no other researcher.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAlexander1987viii_281-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlexander1987viii-281">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The official transcript of Stepinac's trial <i>Suđenje Lisaku, Stepincu</i> etc. was published in Zagreb in 1946, but contains substantial evidence of alteration.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAlexander1987viii_281-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlexander1987viii-281">&#91;277&#93;</a></sup> Alexander's <i>Triple Myth</i> therefore relies on the Yugoslav and foreign press—particularly <i><a href="/wiki/Vjesnik" title="Vjesnik">Vjesnik</a></i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Narodne_Novine" class="mw-redirect" title="Narodne Novine">Narodne Novine</a></i>—as well as <i>Katolički List</i>. All other primary sources available to researchers only indirectly focus on Stepinac.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAlexander1987ix_282-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAlexander1987ix-282">&#91;278&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Aloysius_Stepinac&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1132942124">.mw-parser-output .portalbox{padding:0;margin:0.5em 0;display:table;box-sizing:border-box;max-width:175px;list-style:none}.mw-parser-output .portalborder{border:solid #aaa 1px;padding:0.1em;background:#f9f9f9}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-entry{display:table-row;font-size:85%;line-height:110%;height:1.9em;font-style:italic;font-weight:bold}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-image{display:table-cell;padding:0.2em;vertical-align:middle;text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .portalbox-link{display:table-cell;padding:0.2em 0.2em 0.2em 0.3em;vertical-align:middle}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .portalleft{clear:left;float:left;margin:0.5em 1em 0.5em 0}.mw-parser-output .portalright{clear:right;float:right;margin:0.5em 0 0.5em 1em}}</style><ul role="navigation" aria-label="Portals" class="noprint portalbox portalborder portalright">

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Aloysius_Stepinac&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-16"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-16">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">According to other sources, there were only eight children.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEButlerBurns1995263_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEButlerBurns1995263-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGitman200649_15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGitman200649-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-20"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-20">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">According to one source, Stepinac was awarded the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_Kara%C4%91or%C4%91e%27s_Star" title="Order of Karađorđe&#39;s Star">Order of Karađorđe's Star</a> for his service with the Yugoslav Legion.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERamet2007100_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERamet2007100-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-21"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-21">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">According to one source, Stepinac stayed in Zagreb for five years, studying agriculture and being active in church affairs, including Catholic youth organisations.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEButlerBurns1995263_14-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEButlerBurns1995263-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-22"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-22">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">According to one source, Stepinac fell in love with Marija and proposed, but she called the wedding off, saying they didn't belong together.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEĆorić1998II_17-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEĆorić1998II-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Another source states that Stepinac called off the engagement as he had decided to enter the priesthood.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEButlerBurns1995263_14-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEButlerBurns1995263-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
